# Data.Mutable

A curated mutable-container facade. Region-indexed operations carry Mut r; callback-backed heaps and stable linked-list elements have their own module imports.

<details class="doc-notes"><summary>Module notes</summary>

A curated mutable-container facade with region-indexed operations.
Import Data.Mutable.HeapView for callback-backed heaps and
Data.Mutable.LinkedList for stable, independently retained list elements.

Every container is indexed by a region `r`, and every operation on it
carries the `Mut r` label in its row:

  word-counts :: forall r. List String ->{Mut r} HashTable r String Nat
  word-counts ws =
    let t = hash-table-new () in
    let counted = count-into t ws in
    t

`Mut r` is a capability exactly like `IO r`: a definition whose row
lacks it cannot touch a container of region `r`, so a pure function
stays pure and every function that mutates says so in its type. A
program discharges the label at its entry point
(`main :: Unit ->{Mut r} Int`). Labels need no declaration, so there is
nothing to import for the row itself.

The family (each module's header gives its layout, costs and the
invariant that keeps its reads sound):

  Data.Mutable.Vec        growable vector, introsort      (std::vector)
  Data.Mutable.HashTable  SwissTable/F14 hash table       (absl::flat_hash_map, folly::F14ValueMap)
  Data.Mutable.HashSet    the table's set                 (absl::flat_hash_set)
  Data.Mutable.Deque      power-of-two ring buffer        (Rust VecDeque)
  Data.Mutable.Heap       4-ary priority queue            (boost::d_ary_heap)
  Data.Mutable.Array      fixed-length mutable array      (std::array by reference)
  Data.Mutable.Var        one mutable cell                (IORef, STRef)
  Data.Mutable.BTree      pooled degree-8 B-tree map      (absl::btree_map)
  Data.Mutable.BTreeSet   the B-tree's set                (absl::btree_set)
  Data.Mutable.IndexMap   insertion-ordered hash map      (folly::F14VectorMap, Rust indexmap)
  Data.Mutable.LruCache   bounded LRU cache               (folly::EvictingCacheMap)
  Data.Mutable.BitSet     word-wide dynamic bitset        (boost::dynamic_bitset)
  Data.Mutable.UnionFind  disjoint sets                   (union by size, path halving)

Named refusals (boundaries the family deliberately does not cross):
  a pure runner: `run-mut :: (forall r. Unit ->{Mut r} a) -> a` (the
    `ST` runner) needs a rank-2 argument, which the checker refuses
    ("quantifiers are only supported at the outermost position"), and a
    handler cannot stand in for it, because a handler only dispatches
    operations its handled expression performs directly (decision D23),
    never ones a called container operation performs. Without the
    quantifier a runner would let a container escape the scope that ran
    it and be mutated under a pure type, so none is offered;
  cross-task sharing: a container is not synchronized, so it must stay
    with the task that made it (`Effect.Stm`'s `TVar` is the shared
    mutable cell);
  aliasing views: no container hands out an `Array` that aliases its
    storage; `vec-freeze` and `mut-array-freeze` copy.

</details>

descent skips every subtree wholly below lo, and stops at the first
key at or above hi.

</div>

<article class="doc-entry" id="v-btree-verify">
<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">btree-verify</strong> :: <span class="tok-keyword">forall</span> (<span class="tok-variable">r</span> :: <span class="tok-constructor">Region</span>) <span class="tok-variable">k</span> <span class="tok-variable">v</span>. <span class="tok-keyword">using</span> (<span class="tok-variable">o</span> :: <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Relation.Ord/#c-Ord" title="Relation.Ord.Ord">Ord</a> <span class="tok-variable">k</span>) <span class="tok-operator">=&gt;</span> <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Mutable/#t-BTreeMap" title="Data.Mutable.BTree.BTreeMap">BTreeMap</a> <span class="tok-variable">r</span> <span class="tok-variable">k</span> <span class="tok-variable">v</span> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span>{<a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/reference/10-ownership-and-memory/" title="Mut">Mut</a> r} <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Bool/" title="Bool">Bool</a></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-btree-verify" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Mutable.BTree/#L885" title="View source at line 885">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

The tree's whole structural contract: keys strictly ascending in key
order across nodes, every non-root node holding 7 .. 15 keys, every
leaf at the same depth (the recorded height), and the entry count the
header records. `True` when all of it holds.

</div>
</article>

<article class="doc-entry" id="v-vec-for-each">
<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">vec-for-each</strong> :: <span class="tok-keyword">forall</span> (<span class="tok-variable">r</span> :: <span class="tok-constructor">Region</span>) <span class="tok-variable">a</span>. <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Mutable/#t-Vec" title="Data.Mutable.Vec.Vec">Vec</a> <span class="tok-variable">r</span> <span class="tok-variable">a</span> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span> (<span class="tok-variable">a</span> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span>{<a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/reference/10-ownership-and-memory/" title="Mut">Mut</a> r} <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/reference/04-types-and-kinds/" title="Unit">Unit</a>) <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span>{<a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/reference/10-ownership-and-memory/" title="Mut">Mut</a> r} <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/reference/04-types-and-kinds/" title="Unit">Unit</a></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-vec-for-each" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Mutable.Vec/#L349" title="View source at line 349">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

Run an action on each element in index order. The storage and the
length are re-read at every step, so an action that changes this
vector is memory-safe (it decides what is visited after it).

</div>
</article>

<article class="doc-entry" id="v-vec-freeze">
<div class="doc-sig-bar"><code class="doc-sig"><strong class="doc-decl-name tok-variable">vec-freeze</strong> :: <span class="tok-keyword">forall</span> (<span class="tok-variable">r</span> :: <span class="tok-constructor">Region</span>) <span class="tok-variable">a</span>. <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Mutable/#t-Vec" title="Data.Mutable.Vec.Vec">Vec</a> <span class="tok-variable">r</span> <span class="tok-variable">a</span> <span class="tok-operator">-&gt;</span>{<a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/reference/10-ownership-and-memory/" title="Mut">Mut</a> r} <a class="doc-type-link tok-constructor" href="/library/Data.Array/" title="Array">Array</a> <span class="tok-variable">a</span></code><span class="doc-sig-links"><a href="#v-vec-freeze" title="Permalink">#</a><a href="/library/source/Data.Mutable.Vec/#L397" title="View source at line 397">Source</a></span></div>
<div class="doc-body">

An immutable copy of the elements. The copy is a fresh block with
every slot written, so it is an ordinary `Array a` and no later
write to the vector reaches it.

</div>
</article>
